- **reasoning-least-to-most**: Decompose a hard, multi-step problem into an ordered chain of easier sub-problems and solve them in sequence, feeding each answer into the next, so the final (hardest) step is solved last with all the groundwork already done. Trigger for multi-hop questions, compositional math/logic problems, nested planning tasks, or any problem that is too big to answer correctly in one hop — especially with a weaker/cheaper model that tends to skip steps or drop terms when asked to jump straight to the answer. ([firststone-vc/reasoning-pack](https://github.com/firststone-vc/reasoning-pack/tree/HEAD/skills/reasoning-least-to-most) · ★1)

([Eman-Yousaf/AI_employee](https://github.com/Eman-Yousaf/AI_employee/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/reasoning-loop) · ★1)
- **reasoning-model-prompting**: Use when prompting a reasoning model — OpenAI o-series (o3, o4-mini, o5), GPT-5 with reasoning_effort, or Anthropic Claude with extended thinking enabled. - **reasoning-reflexion**: Use after producing any draft with a right/wrong dimension (code, math, structured extraction, factual analysis) and before it ships — runs a dedicated critique pass that hunts for errors in the draft, then a separate correction pass that fixes only what the critique found. - **reasoning-router**: Classify problems and route to optimal thinking MCP (11 available). Monitor confidence, re-route on stall. Use for complex reasoning. - **reasoning-rubric-grader**: Use before shipping any deliverable that has checkable success criteria (code diffs, data extraction, structured reports, answers with hard constraints like word limits or required citations) — define an explicit scoring rubric, grade the draft against it with evidence, and only deliver once it clears a stated bar; otherwise revise and re-grade (bounded rounds). Skip for purely subjective/creative tasks with no checkable criteria. ([firststone-vc/reasoning-pack](https://github.com/firststone-vc/reasoning-pack/tree/HEAD/skills/reasoning-rubric-grader) · ★1)

- **reasoning-swarm**: Use when a decision or proposal has genuine multi-stakeholder stakes, spans multiple teams, or carries organization-scale or hard-to-reverse consequences — build-vs-buy calls, architecture decisions affecting several teams, policy with systemic impact, or when the user explicitly wants independent parallel takes stress-tested against each other rather than one recursive pass.
